Evangelium secundum Lucam
by Anselm of Laon and his school

## PROLOGUE OF BLESSED JEROME.

[n: ( Vide inter ejus opera. )]

## PROEM OF LUKE ON HIS OWN GOSPEL.

VERS. 1.-- *Forasmuch indeed,* etc. [n: (BEDA.)] Luke, concerning all the things which Jesus did and taught, etc., *as far as* they were setting a narrative in order rather than weaving the truth of history.

[0243D]VERS. 3.-- *All things. [n: (ID.)]* Not all the things which he had attained, but out of all those which he judges apt for confirming the faith of his readers. For of set purpose he passes over many things which the others tell, so that a varied grace might shine out in the Gospel, and that in the several books the marvels of the mysteries and of the deeds might stand out, each by certain things of its own. *Theophilus.* Theophilus, one loving God, etc., *as far as* he ought to know the faith of the everlasting Godhead and the order of his dispensation in time.



## CHAPTER ONE.

VERS. 5.-- *There was in the days of Herod, the king of Judaea. [n: (AMBR.)]* The calf is the priestly victim. By the calf, therefore, this Gospel is figured, in which the beginning is made from the priests and the end is made in the calf, that is, in Christ, who is offered up for the life of the world.

*A certain priest.* He has filled out the order: he has named the king, and the region in which he was; he points out the priest and his nation and his wife. *Zacharias.* Zacharias, mindful of the Lord: to whom an Angel appears at the right of the altar, because to him who is mindful of the Lord the mystery is shown.

*Of the course of Abia. [n: (BEDA.)]* Abia, the Lord is Father, etc., [0244D] *as far as* just as by the seven the Old Testament is declared.

*Elizabeth.* Elizabeth, the fullness of my God, signifies Mary, who was full of God.

VERS. 6.-- *And they were both. [n: (BEDA, AMBR.)]* A full praise, which comprehends birth in his forebears, character in fairness, office in the priesthood, deed in the commandments, judgment in the justifications. *Just before God. [n: (AMBR.)]* For not everyone who is just before men is just before God; for men see in one way, God in another: men in the face, God in the heart. And therefore it can come about that someone, by an affected popular goodness, should seem just to me, and yet not be just before God, if his justice is not formed out of simplicity of mind but is feigned by flattery.

[0245A]VERS. 7.-- *And there was not unto them. [n: (BEDA.)]* It was divinely provided that John should be born of parents advanced in years and long deprived of the fruit of wedlock, so that by the unlooked-for birth of the child the gift of God might touch them the more gratefully, and the astonishment of the miracle might prepare the rest for the hearing of the prophecy to come. Whence afterwards it is added: *All they that had heard laid it up in their heart, saying: What manner of child, thinkest thou, shall this be?*

VERS. 8.-- *And it came to pass.* Whereas by the precept of Moses, when one priest died, one is bidden to succeed him, in David's time it was appointed that, ministering by turns for eight days at a time from sabbath to sabbath in the time of their course, they should each give themselves to chastity, and should not meanwhile touch their house.

VERS. 9.-- *By lot. [n: (BEDA.)]* He was not then chosen by a new lot, when the incense was to be burned, etc., *as far [0245B] as* he is foretold while that other is promised as one who will go before.

VERS. 12.-- *And Zacharias was troubled at the sight, and fear fell upon him,* etc. [n: (AMBR.)] We are wont to be troubled and estranged from our own feeling when we are shaken by the encounter of a higher power. But as it belongs to human weakness to be terrified, so it belongs to angelic kindness to console with soothing one who is afraid at its appearing; on the contrary, the demons, if they have perceived any terrified by their presence, shake them with a larger horror.

VERS. 13.-- *Because thy prayer is heard: and thy wife Elizabeth shall bear thee a son, and thou shalt call his name.* It was heard for more than thou didst ask. Thou didst pray for the deliverance of the people, and a forerunner is given thee.

[n: (AMBR.)] Many divine benefits are heaped together here. First, the fruit of the prayer; then the childbearing of a barren woman. [0245C] Then the gladness of very many, and greatness of virtue, and even the prophecy of the Most High is promised. Moreover, that there might be no doubt, the name of the child to come is appointed. Since therefore so many things flow beyond what he had prayed for, not undeservedly is his diffidence punished with the penalty of silence.

VERS. 14.-- *And there shall be joy,* etc. [n: (BEDA.)] It is a token of singular merit as often as a name is either imposed upon men by God or changed, etc., *as far as* because this one preaches salvation to many, but that one gives it to all. *[n: (AMBR.)] Joy and exultation.* The parents are admonished to give thanks no less, etc., *as far as* mothers, because they are honoured with the rewards of wedlock.

VERS. 15.-- *Wine and strong drink,* etc. [n: (BEDA.)] It is of great virtue before God to preach heavenly joys in the desert, etc., *as far as* and already there was the spirit of grace.

[0245D]VERS. 16.-- *And he shall convert many of the children of Israel. [n: (ID.)]* Since John, bearing witness to Christ and baptizing in the faith of Christ, is said to have converted the children of Israel to God, it is plain that Christ is the Lord God of Israel.

VERS. 17.-- *He shall go before. [n: (ID.)]* As Elias is the herald of the judge, etc., *as far as* he is made manifest in glory with him. *In the spirit and power. [n: (AMBR.)]* Spirit and power are rightly joined. For there is no spirit without power, nor power without spirit. Elias had the power of abstinence, and of patience, and of turning the minds of peoples from faithlessness to faith; and the spirit of prophesying he had.

*That he may turn,* etc., [n: (BEDA.)] He turns the hearts of the fathers unto the children, [0246A] etc., *as far as* almost the same words which the angel foretold of John, Malachi foretold of Elias.

VERS. 18.-- *Whereby,* etc. [n: (ID.)] If it were a man who was promising, it would be allowable to seek a sign unpunished, etc., *as far as* and it would be the penalty of unbelief which he deserved.

VERS. 19.-- *Before God. [n: (ID.)]* When angels come to us, they so fulfil their ministry outwardly that nevertheless they stand within, before God, by contemplation. For although an angel is a circumscribed spirit, the highest Spirit, who is God, is uncircumscribed, and within him the angel runs wherever he is moved or sent.

VERS. 22.-- *And he was making signs to them, and remained dumb,* etc. Labouring to indicate bodily acts without a voice, and not expressing his will. Like to him is the people of the Jews, [0246B] unable to render an account of their own acts.

VERS. 23.-- *When they were fulfilled,* etc. Because in the time of their course the priests of the temple, given over to its offices alone, abstained not only from the embrace of their wives but even from entering their houses. But to our priests, from whom not a carnal succession but a spiritual perfection is sought, who ought to be daily at hand at the altar, perpetual chastity is enjoined.

VERS. 24.-- *She hid herself. [n: (AMBR.)]* She hides herself because she blushes at the age of her childbearing, etc., *as far as* for neither would she who would not blush at intercourse in old age blush at the childbearing.

VERS. 25.-- *Because thus hath he done. [n: (BEDA.)]* These things were done, says John Chrysostom, etc., *as far as* because through him the affliction of penance was to be preached.

VERS. 26.-- *And in the sixth month. [n: (HIER.)]* As in the sixth [0246C] month the angel Gabriel was sent by God into a city of Galilee, so in the sixth millennium Christ was sent,

*Gabriel. [n: (BEDA.)]* For this reason angels are sometimes marked out by a name, so that from the name itself it may be shown what they come to minister. Gabriel is the strength of God, because he announces him who was coming to war down the devil.

VERS. 27.-- *To a virgin.* A woman seduced by the devil brought in death: on the contrary, a woman taught by an angel brought forth salvation.

VERS. 28.-- *The Lord is with thee.* Whom by a new love of chastity he raised up to heavenly things, and afterwards, human nature mediating, consecrated with all the fullness of the Godhead. *Blessed art thou among women.* Who, without example in the condition of women, is both virgin and mother, and brought forth God.

VERS. 29.-- *She was troubled,* etc. Because she did not know whether [0246D] what the angel said was past or to come.

VERS. 30.-- *And the angel said to her: Fear not, Mary.* Her, troubled by the unwonted salutation, he calls by name as one more familiarly known. He bids her not to fear, and more fully explains why he had called her full of grace.

VERS. 31.-- *Jesus.* Jesus is called saviour, or salutary. For he himself shall save, not the people of Israel, but his own people, whether from the uncircumcision or from the circumcision, gathered into one fold under one shepherd.

VERS. 32.-- *The throne of David his father. [n: (BEDA.)]* That is, the Israelite people, etc., *as far as* in those who, cut from the wild olive, have been grafted into the good olive.

VERS. 35.-- *The Holy One shall be called the Son of God.* Jesus is born holy, who was to overcome the condition of corruptible nature, [0247A] and he was not conceived by the mingling of carnal union. We, bound fast by the condition of corruptible nature, can be sanctified through grace. And it is fitting that she who conceived as a virgin against custom should bring forth the Son of God above the custom of human usage.

VERS. 36.-- *And behold,* etc. [n: (BEDA.)] Lest the virgin should despair of the childbearing, etc., *as far as* she might know that the forerunner too was to be born of a barren old woman.

*Thy cousin.* Because she was of the tribe of Levi, that is, of the tribe of God.

[n: (BEDA.)] We read that Aaron, who was of the tribe of Levi, etc., *as far as* which according to God did not lack the fellowship of a spiritual kinship.

VERS. 38.-- *Behold the handmaid of the Lord.* She does not exalt herself for the singularity of her merit, but, mindful in all things of her own condition and of the divine kinship, she confesses herself to be the handmaid [0247B] of him whose mother she is chosen to be, and with great devotion desires the angel's promise to be fulfilled.

VERS. 39.-- *And Mary rising up in those days, went into the hill country with haste into a city of Juda. [n: (AMBR.)]* Not incredulous of the oracle, etc., *as far as* when it is added: *And it came to pass, that when she heard.*

VERS. 40.-- *And she entered into the house of Zachary.* From this let holy women learn what attentiveness they ought to show to kinswomen with child.

*And saluted Elizabeth. [n: (BEDA.)]* She salutes first, because it is fitting that the chaster she is, the humbler she should be.

VERS. 41.-- *He exulted,* etc. Because he could not with the tongue, he salutes with an exulting spirit, and begins the office of his forerunning. Behold, there appears what the angel had said: *He shall be filled with the Holy Ghost even from his mother's womb.*

[0247C]VERS. 42.-- *Blessed art thou. [n: (BEDA, AMBR.)]* The words of the Gospel agree with the words of the psalm in which it is promised to David: *Of the fruit of thy womb I will set upon thy throne [n: (Psal. CXXXI)]*. And Mary is blessed by Elizabeth with the same voice as by Gabriel, so that she may be shown venerable both to angels and to men, and to be preferred before our women.

VERS. 43.-- *The mother of the Lord.* I perceive the miracle, I acknowledge the mystery: the mother of the Lord, teeming with the Word, full of God.

VERS. 44.-- *As was made.* In the very moment in which the voice enters the ears of the body, a spiritual power enters the heart of the hearer, and kindles not only the mother but also the offspring with love of her who comes.

*He exulted. [n: (BEDA.)]* By the revealing of the Spirit with whom she was filled, etc., *as far as* the virgin believed where the priest denied.

[0247D]VERS. 46.-- *My soul doth magnify the Lord. [n: (ID.)]* First she professes the gifts spiritually granted to her, etc., *as far as* I spend in the keeping of his commandments.

VERS. 47.-- *And hath exulted. [n: (ID.)] [n: (ID.)]* Because I do not care for earthly things, prosperity does not soften me, adversity does not break me, but the memory of him alone delights me, from whom eternal salvation is hoped for; I rejoice in the Godhead of him by whose conception in time my flesh is made fruitful.

VERS. 48.-- *Because he hath regarded. [n: (ID.)]* Behold how lowly are the things she feels concerning herself, etc., *as far as* and out of the small and the weak he makes them great and strong.

*Because he hath done great things to me.* Because in me he took flesh, and cleansed me from sins, and conferred upon me the gifts of the Holy Ghost.

[0248A] *And mercy. [n: (BEDA.)]* From the special gifts she turns herself to the general judgments of God, etc., *as far as* he adds what the despisers deserve.

VERS. 53.-- *He hath filled the hungry with good things. [n: (ID.)]* They who desire eternal things with all zeal; shall be filled, when Christ shall have appeared in glory. But they who rejoice in earthly things are sent away at the last empty of all blessedness. In the present life also we see the humble filled with divine grace, and the proud deprived of the light of truth.

VERS. 55.-- *As he spoke to our fathers.* Making mention of the fathers to whom this salvation was revealed, she names Abraham expressly, to whom the incarnation itself was first made manifest. *And to his seed for ever.* Not so much to those born of the flesh as to those who have followed the footsteps of faith, to whom the coming of the Saviour was promised for ever, [0248B] because the promise of the inheritance itself shall be closed by no end. For both unto the end of the world believers shall not be lacking, and the glory of blessedness shall be everlasting.



## CHAPTER ONE. (cont.)

VERS. 56.-- *And Mary abode. [n: (BEDA.)]* She abode so long, until she should see the nativity of the forerunner, for the sake of which above all she had come. Nor did she abide so long for the sake of familiarity alone, but that in so long a time the advancement both of Elizabeth and of John might grow. For if at Mary's first coming they were filled with the Holy Ghost, much was added to them over and above by so long a delay of her stay.

VERS. 59.-- *On the eighth day.* By circumcision the resurrection of the Lord is figured, because it too was made on the eighth day, that is, after the sabbath. And as the one was wont to absolve from [0248C] the guilt of death, so the other both exhibited in our author the birth of immortal life, and showed that it is to be hoped for in us.

*And they called him. [n: (BEDA.)]* The celebrated nativity of John is the grace of the New Testament begun, etc., *as far as* it expresses the grace of the Gospel, if it be rightly understood.

VERS. 62.-- *And they made signs to his father,* etc. They who make signs to the father about the child's name signify those who by the testimony of the law aim to build up the grace of faith.

VERS. 67.-- *And he prophesied, saying. [n: (BEDA.)]* Great is the bounty of the divine mercy. Behold, the speech which alone was taken away from him while he doubted is restored, together with the spirit of prophecy, to him now believing.

VERS. 68.-- *Blessed.* Who, while he besought the goodness of God, [0248D] was given a forerunner in place of deliverance.

*He hath visited.* What he had known was shortly to be done, he relates after the manner of the prophets as though it were already done.

VERS. 69.-- *A horn of salvation to us. [n: (BEDA.)]* By the horn, which rises above the flesh, since all the bones are covered with flesh, the kingdom of Christ is signified, by which the world and the joys of the flesh are overcome. In figure of which David and Solomon were consecrated to the glory of the kingdom with a horn of oil, as Saul before them with a vial.

VERS. 71.-- *Salvation from our enemies, and from the hand,* etc. He hath raised up for us salvation from our enemies; and it is an explanation of the earlier verse, in which he had briefly set the same thing before: *And he hath raised up a horn of salvation to us.*

VERS. 72.-- *To perform mercy to [0249A] our fathers, and to remember his testament,* etc. He hath spoken, and hath raised up salvation for us, to perform mercy: that the mercy which he promised to the fathers he may fulfil in us, and that the oath which he made to the fathers concerning our deliverance may be accomplished in us through Christ.

VERS. 76.-- *And thou, child, shalt be called the prophet of the Highest: for thou shalt go before the face of the Lord to prepare,* etc. [n: (BEDA.)] It is no wonder if he addresses an infant of eight days, etc., *as far as* which he acknowledged that he had received among his sons.

VERS. 79, 80.-- *To direct our feet into the way of peace. And the child grew,* etc. That in all things the steps of our works may accord with the grace of him who enlightens us, until we enter into the dwelling of perpetual peace.



## CHAPTER II.

[0249B]

VERS. 1.-- *There went out a decree from Caesar. [n: (AUG., BED.)]* Augustus reigned in peace for twelve years about the nativity of Christ, etc., *as far as* here first Judaea was made tributary to the Romans.

VERS. 2.-- *The first. [n: (BEDA.)]* Already at various times very many parts of the world are read to have been enrolled, but this is the first of those which took in the whole world; and it is a profession of minds, because no one is shut out. Or certainly it then first began, when Cyrinus was sent by Caesar into Syria, to be an assessor of patrimonies.

VERS. 4.-- *Nazareth. [n: (ID.)]* Daily the Lord is conceived in Nazareth, etc., *as far as* the insistence of the teacher who speaks avails nothing unless it has received the increase of help from above [0249C] that it may be heard.

VERS. 6.-- *And it came to pass.* The Lord willed to be conceived in one place and born in another, that he might the more easily escape the fury of the plotting Herod. The humility of Christ is commended, because he willed not only to be made flesh, but even to be born at that time in which, as soon as he was born, he should be made subject to Caesar's census for the sake of our deliverance.

VERS. 7.-- *She wrapped him up in swaddling clothes.* He is clothed in mean cloths, that we might receive the robe of immortality. His hands and feet are bound fast, that our hands may be directed to working well, and our feet into the way of peace; he was made a little one, that we might be perfect.

VERS. 8.-- *And there were shepherds. [n: (BEDA.)]* The highest shepherd being born, the shepherds from the snares of the night, etc., *as far as* upon whom the divine grace flashes more abundantly.

[0249D]VERS. 9.-- *And behold an angel of the Lord. [n: (ID.)]* The citizens of heaven bear witness to the Lord both as to be conceived, and as conceived, and as born, that they may both sufficiently instruct mortals and pay their own service to their author.

VERS. 12.-- *An infant wrapped in swaddling clothes. [n: (ID.)]* The infancy of the Saviour has been pressed home by frequent testimonies both of the angels and of the evangelists, that it may be fixed the more deeply in our minds what he was made for our sake: in which his humility is commended: *Because, whereas he was rich, he became poor for us, that by his own poverty he might enrich us [n: (II Cor)]*. [var: Vulg. 2 Cor 8:9 reads *propter vos egenus factus est*, "for your sakes he became needy," and *ut illius inopia vos divites essetis*, "that by his poverty you might be rich"; Migne's clause has *pauper* for *egenus* and puts in the first person what the Vulgate puts in the second.]

VERS. 14.-- *And on earth peace.* When by sinning we were strangers to God, the angels reckoned us strangers; but because we have known our king, the angels [0250A] have recognized us as their own fellow citizens. And the angel fears to be adored by a human nature which he beholds in his own king.

*To men of good will. [n: (BEDA.)]* Who receive Christ when born; not to those who at the hearing of his nativity were troubled and persecuted him. *There is no peace to the wicked, saith the Lord [n: (Isa. XLVIII)]*.

VERS. 16.-- *And they came with haste. [n: (BEDA.)]* The presence of Christ is not to be sought with sloth, etc., *as far as* they will find him as it were in a manger.

VERS. 17.-- *And seeing.* It is the vision or knowledge of God, and this alone is the life of men, that they should know the true Father, and that Jesus Christ, whom he sent, is one God.

VERS. 18.-- *They wondered.* They wonder both at the mystery of the incarnation and at so great a testimony of shepherds, who [0250B] did not know how to feign what they had heard, but with a simple eloquence proclaimed true things. But the Lord appointed not orators but fishermen to preach the Gospel. So too in the Old Testament he ordained shepherds as the messengers of his dispensation. Therefore the testimony of the shepherds is not to be lightly esteemed.

VERS. 20.-- *And returning,* etc. [n: (BEDA.)] Thus spiritual shepherds, while others sleep, are borne up by contemplating heavenly things, etc., *as far as* when they say: *Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace to men of good will.*

VERS. 22.-- *They carried him.* The little ones who from the whole kingdom of the Jews were carried to the temple, if they were of the tribe of Levi, were kept back perpetually for the ministry of the house. If of another tribe, the firstborn were redeemed by a price given by their parents, to be carried back to their own homes.

[0250C]VERS. 23.-- *As it is written. [n: (BEDA.)]* It is written in the law: *A woman who, having received,* etc., *as far as* she enters upon the joys of heavenly blessedness with the fruits of good works.

VERS. 24.-- *A pair of turtledoves. [n: (ID.)]* This was the offering of the poor, who were not able to offer a lamb, so that in all things the humility of the Lord might be plain.

VERS. 25.-- *And behold there was a man.* Not only the angels, but every age and sex render testimony to the child that is born. And as he was foretold by the faithful of all ages, so at his coming he is proclaimed with the praise of all the saints.

VERS. 27.-- *The child.* Since boyhood begins after the seven years of infancy, Jesus is frequently called a child, [0250D] not so much for his age as for his service; whence the Prophet: *Behold my child,* [var: Vulg. Isai 42:1 reads *Ecce servus meus*, "Behold my servant"; the gloss's whole argument turns on *puer*.] because *the Son of man came not to be ministered unto, but to minister [n: (Isa. XLII)].*

VERS. 29.-- *Now thou dost dismiss thy servant. [n: (AMBR.)]* He who wishes to be dismissed, let him come into Jerusalem, etc., *as far as* then he shall be dismissed so that he may not see death, because he had seen life.

VERS. 34.-- *Behold this child is set.* For the ruin of those who had stood, and the resurrection of those who had fallen; or for the ruin of vices, and for the resurrection of virtues. Not in himself alone, but in his preachers also is he set for ruin and resurrection; whence the Apostle: *We are the good odour of Christ unto God, in them that are saved, and in them that perish.* For by the good odour some are saved, others perish [n: (II Cor. II)].

[0251A]VERS. 35.-- *A sword. [n: (BEDA.)]* The sorrow of the Lord's passion, etc., *as far as* she beholds the cockle springing up.

*That may be revealed.* Before, it was uncertain which of the Jews would receive the grace of Christ, and which would spit upon it: but at the hearing of the nativity the thoughts are revealed, and Herod and others are troubled, while certain men come to the teaching of Christ.

VERS. 36.-- *And there was Anna,* etc. Because through a man and a woman the life of the whole world was lost, in the testimony of life returning through Christ Anna is joined to Simeon. It agrees with the mysteries of the Church that Anna is interpreted grace, who is the daughter of Phanuel, who is called the face of God; whence above: *The light of thy countenance, O Lord, is signed upon us [n: (Psal. IV)]*. *Of the tribe of Aser.* That is, of the blessed, she is descended, from him who among the twelve [0251B] patriarchs is eighth in the order of birth, on account of the mystery of the resurrection. There are five Annas. The first the mother of Samuel, the second the wife of Raguel, the third the mother of Tobias, the fourth the daughter of Phanuel, the fifth the mother of Mary.

*She had advanced in days. [n: (BEDA.)]* According to the history, Anna, and of a devout manner of life, etc., *as far as* they are multiplied on account of the apostolic teaching.

VERS. 38.-- *To all who,* etc. To all the faithful who, weighed down by the yoke of the foreigner Herod, were awaiting the deliverance of the city and of the people, she promised through the coming of Christ a redemption close at hand from the tyranny of the stranger.

VERS. 40.-- *And the child. [n: (BEDA.)]* In that he was a child, etc., *as far as* he had begun to be made man, he was perfect [0251C] as God.

VERS. 41.-- *And his parents went,* etc. [n: (ID.)] Deservedly Luke is compared, among the four living creatures, to the calf, etc., *as far as* at the end of his Gospel he concludes with the disciples of the Lord praising in the temple.

VERS. 45.-- *And not finding him. [n: (ID.)]* If it is asked how he could be left behind by his parents through forgetfulness, etc., *as far as* returned with the other parent.

VERS. 46.-- *In the midst. [n: (ID.)]* As a fountain, he sits in the midst of the doctors; but as a pattern of humility, he asks and hears before he instructs, lest little ones should blush to be taught by their elders, lest one who is weak should dare to teach.

VERS. 49.-- *How is it that you sought me? [n: (ID.)]* He does not find fault with their seeking him as a son, but [0251D] he suggests who is his true Father, and compels them to lift the eyes of the mind to what is rather owed to him whose eternal Son he is. For because he is God and man, now he sets forth the lofty things of the Godhead, now the weak things of the humanity. As the Son of God he stays in the temple; as the Son of man he goes back with his parents whither they bid.

VERS. 51.-- *And his mother kept all these words in her heart.* All the things which she knew to have been done concerning the Lord or by the Lord, whether those she understood or those she could not yet understand, she laid up all in her memory, so that, when the time should come for his incarnation to be preached or written, she might be able sufficiently to set forth to those who asked all things just as they had been done. So let us, by frequent meditation on the deeds and words of the Lord, [0252A] drive off importunate thoughts and labour to instruct others.

VERS. 52.-- *He advanced in wisdom. [n: (BEDA.)]* As it belongs to the flesh to advance in age, etc., *as far as* both alike might be saved.



## CHAPTER III.

VERS. 2.-- *The word was made,* etc. [n: (BEDA.)] About to gather the Church, the Son of God works first in his little servant, etc., *as far as* he therefore said one thing, and declared all.

VERS. 3.-- *Preaching the baptism of penance,* etc. [n: (ID.)] John preached the baptism of penance, and even gave it to some, etc., *as far as* there will be a going before by his own baptism, by which sins cannot be loosed.

VERS. 4.-- *The voice of one crying.* A voice, because he is the herald [0252B] of the Word. *In the desert.* Because he announces the consolation of redemption to a deserted and forsaken Judaea. No word is heard without a voice, nor does a voice avail without the understanding of the word. *Prepare ye the way of the Lord,* etc. He who preaches faith and good works, what else than for the Lord who comes? He prepares a way to the hearts of his hearers, that this force of grace may give light, that it may make straight paths for God, while by the word of preaching it forms clean thoughts in the mind.

VERS. 5.-- *And the crooked shall be. [n: (BEDA.)]* The crooked, that is, the hearts of the wicked, twisted by injustice, shall be directed to the rule of justice.

VERS. 6.-- *And shall see. [n: (ID.)]* In this life not every man could see Christ, but on the day of judgment, on the seat of majesty, both the elect and the reprobate alike shall see him, that [0252C] the just may be rewarded and the wicked groan for ever. Whence there is also added:

VERS. 7.-- *Who hath shewed,* etc.? [n: (AMBR.)] Who hath shewed, in the prudence poured in by the mercy of God, etc.? *as far as* which he prophesies is to be transferred to the Gentiles.

VERS. 8.-- *For I say to you. [n: (ID.)]* They who, having lost the sense of reason, etc., *as far as* they are filled with fruits, they are clothed with leaves.

VERS. 11.-- *He that hath two. (BEDA)*. The tunic is more necessary of itself than the cloak, etc., *as far as* but let him share what he has with the poor man.

VERS. 12.-- *The publicans. [n: (ID.)]* They who exact the public tolls, etc., *as far as* they might at length attain to sharing their own goods with their neighbours.

VERS. 15.-- *And while they were supposing. [n: (ID.)]* Not only [0252D] were they thinking, etc., *as far as* they do not believe that he came through a virgin.

VERS. 16.-- *But there shall come.* Therefore I baptize with water, because he comes who will baptize with the Spirit, and it is fitting that a way be prepared for him.

*Whose I am not worthy. [n: (BEDA.)]* I am not worthy to search out the mystery of the incarnation, etc., *as far as* the evangelical preaching, with which the apostles are shod.

VERS. 19.-- *Herod.* Before Luke relates anything of the acts of Jesus, he says that John was seized by Herod, in order to show that he will describe only those deeds which were done in that year in which John was either seized or punished.

VERS. 21.-- *And Jesus being baptized. [n: (BEDA.)]* The Lord is baptized, [0253A] not needing to be cleansed, etc., *as far as* now taught, he openly preaches the Son of God.

VERS. 22.-- *In thee I am well pleased.* Not what is another's in the Son, but his own, is praised, as though he should say: Whatsoever things thou hast are mine.

VERS. 23.-- *Thirty years of age. [n: (BEDA.)]* That he is baptized in his thirtieth year, etc., *as far as* as in Jeremias and Daniel.

VERS. 26.-- *Who was of Mathathias, who was of Amos, who was of Nahum, who was of Hesli. [n: (BEDA.)]* Mathath, who is descended through Solomon, etc., *as far as* who through carnal generation is shown to have been made the Son of man.

VERS. 29.-- *Who was of Levi.* But these four who are brought in in this place were not the sons of Jacob, but they shared in their names.

[0253B] [n: (BEDA.)] As if alluding by the names to what was figured in them, etc., *as far as* diverse kinds of virtues might go before.

VERS. 31.-- *Nathan. [n: (ID.)]* That Matthew, through Solomon, etc., *as far as* to whom judgment is given by God the king: *He is a priest for ever according to the order of Melchisedech.* [var: Vulg. Psal 109:4 opens the clause *Tu es sacerdos*, "Thou art a priest"; Migne reports in the third person what the psalm addresses in the second.]

VERS. 37.-- *Mathusale. [n: (AMBR.)]* The years of Mathusale are numbered beyond the flood; it signifies Christ, whose life knows not age.

*Who was of Enoch. [n: (BEDA.)]* Ascending from the baptized Son of God as far as God the Father, he sets Enoch at the seventieth step, etc., *as far as* whom he shows to be joined, in the resurrection, through the ages, [0253C] to the contemplating of the unchangeable wisdom of God.

VERS. 38.-- *Seth.* Seth, the later son of Adam, is not passed over in silence, so that, since there are two generations of the people, it may signify that Christ is to be numbered in the later rather than in the earlier generation.

*Who was of God.* The generation begins from the Son of God, and is ended in the Son of God; the created goes before in figure, that the born may follow in truth; he who was made to the image goes first, that for his sake the image may come down.



## CHAPTER IV.

VERS. 1.-- *And he was led. [n: (AMBR.)]* He was led into the desert with this purpose, that he might provoke the devil; for unless that one had contended, this one would not have conquered for me; with this mystery, [0253D] that he might free Adam from exile; with this example, that he might show us that the devil envies those who reach out toward better things.

*Into the desert.* Historically: into that desert which is between Jerusalem and Jericho, where he had figuratively said that Adam had been overcome by the devil.

VERS. 2.-- *He was hungry. [n: (BEDA.)]* It is not written of Moses or of Elias, etc., *as far as* but he hungers not so much for the food of the body as for the salvation of the soul.

VERS. 3.-- *If thou be the Son of God. [n: (AMBR.)]* He had known that the Son of God would come, but he did not think that he had come through the weakness of the body, etc., *as far as* he who is not fed with this word does not live.

VERS. 4.-- *It is written.* He teaches that one must fight rather with doctrine than with miracles, with humility rather than with power, [0254A] and that nothing is to be done at the devil's pleasure, nor out of any regard for displaying his power.

VERS. 5.-- *And he brought him.* Luke follows the thing as it was done, setting avarice in the middle and pride last, according to the manners of the present time, because pride rages after all the virtues. Matthew does not follow the order of the history, but that of the temptations of Adam.

VERS. 6.-- *To thee will I give.* He says these things out of arrogance, not because the whole world is his, for there are some good men. *It is delivered to me.* Power is not from the devil, but it is nevertheless exposed to the snares of the devil. Nor is the ordering of the powers evil on that account, because the powers are exposed to evil.

VERS. 7.-- *Thou therefore. [n: (BEDA.)]* The devil, saying to the Saviour: *If falling down thou wilt adore me,* hears in return that he himself ought rather to adore him, his Lord and [0254B] his God.

VERS. 8.-- *If thou be the Son of God.* In all the temptations he does this, that he may learn whether he be the Son of God. But Christ so tempers his answer as to leave it in doubt: *Cast thyself down.* Him whom neither gluttony nor avarice overcame, vainglory tempts, in case perhaps the very boasting of his own victory might avail to cast him down. *Cast.* This word befits him who busies himself to hurl all men headlong. In which he is shown to be weak, since he can harm no one unless that one has first cast himself down.

VERS. 9.-- *It is written.* Because he had put forward an example from the Scriptures, he is overcome by examples from the Scriptures. But the devil uses the testimonies of the Scriptures through the heretics, not to teach, but to deceive.

VERS. 10.-- *To the angels. [n: (BEDA.)]* This prophecy is not [0254C] of Christ, but of a holy man. And even if it were of Christ, he ought also to have remembered what follows: *Thou shalt walk upon the asp and the basilisk, and thou shalt trample under foot the lion and the dragon [n: (Psal. XC)]*. But of the help of the angels he speaks as though to one who is weak; of his own treading down he keeps silence, like a shuffler.

VERS. 13.-- *And being ended. [n: (ID.)]* The three temptations being overcome, he says that every temptation was ended, etc., *as far as* yet he dreads to press on, lest he be more often triumphed over.

VERS. 14.-- *And the fame went out.* He is honoured not only by those present because of the miracles they had seen, but among the absent his fame was being spread abroad.

VERS. 15.-- *He taught.* After the miracles, behold wisdom, by which he teaches, and by which teaching, through the preceding miracles, [0254D] he magnifies.

VERS. 16.-- *And he rose up to read. [n: (BEDA.)]* He who had come not to be ministered unto but to minister, etc., *as far as* or he might correct them by the testimony of the reading of the prophecy.

VERS. 18.-- *The Spirit. [n: (ID.)]* In the earlier parts of the prophecy the Saviour, speaking of the calling of the Gentiles and the confirmation of the Church, etc., *as far as* because the Spirit of the Lord is upon him.

*To preach the gospel to the poor,* etc. He understands the Gentiles, who were truly poor. For they had neither the knowledge of God, nor the law, nor the prophets, nor anything else among their goods, and they were destitute of all the other spiritual riches.

VERS. 20.-- *And when he had folded. [n: (BEDA.)]* He read in the hearing [0255A] of those who were present, etc., *as far as* but according to the capacity of the hearers he commits the word to the teacher to be dispensed.

VERS. 23.-- *Physician.* As [they call him] the son of a carpenter, so by the same madness of error; they call him a physician, but in their error the truth lies hidden. For he was truly the son of a carpenter: of one who through him made all things in the beginning, who works in the Holy Ghost and in fire, who in the great house of this world makes vessels of diverse kinds. He is a physician also, because through him all things that are in the heavens and that are on earth have been restored. Who says of himself: *They that are in health need not a physician, but they that are ill [n: (Matth. IX)]*.

VERS. 24.-- *Amen I say to you. [n: (BEDA.)]* Not only the Lord and head of the prophets, etc., *as far as* he turned away the miracles of his power.

VERS. 25.-- *In the days of Elias. [n: (ID.)]* As if he should say: It is not [0255B] contrary to the example of the prophets, etc., *as far as* because there was no abundance for the knowing of the Godhead.

VERS. 26.-- *A widow. [n: (ID.)]* The widow to whom Elias is sent figures the Church of the Gentiles, etc., *as far as* in which the bread of eternal life is prepared for us.

VERS. 27.-- *Naaman the Syrian. [n: (ID.)]* Naaman, who is interpreted comeliness, signifies the people of the nations, etc., *as far as* because it behoves the baptized to be confirmed by partaking of the Lord's body.

VERS. 29.-- *And they led him. [n: (ID.)]* The Jews, worse disciples, with the devil for their master, etc., *as far as* they are preserved as though shaped by the hand of the divine Sculptor.

VERS. 31.-- *And he went down,* etc. [n: (ID.)] Neither moved by indignation, nor offended by their crime, etc., *as far as* nor is the law dissolved, if there be a renewal of a man now falling.

[0255C]VERS. 33.-- *And in the synagogue. [n: (ID.)]* In the synagogue there is a man having an unclean spirit, because he had lost the Holy Spirit. For the devil had entered in, whence Christ had spiritually gone out.

VERS. 34.-- *I know thee.* By his works he could be proved the Son of God. But did he not say to the Apostles: *Greater things shall you do?* The solution: by works, that is, by the manner of the working. Others were saying: *In the name of Jesus,* etc. He himself was saying: *I have said to thee,* etc.

VERS. 35.-- *And when he had thrown him. [n: (BEDA.)]* He who was to be cured is allowed to be thrown into the midst by the demon, so that the power laid open may invite more to salvation.

VERS. 36.-- *And with power.* Not as men, who cast out demons in the word of God, but by his own authority [0255D] he works mighty deeds.

VERS. 38.-- *Into Simon's house.* The house of Peter is the circumcision committed to his apostolate. The mother-in-law is the Synagogue, which is in a manner the mother of the Church committed to Peter. She is sick of a fever, because she laboured under the heats of envy, persecuting the Church. But Jesus, standing over her, heals her, because where the fear of God begins to be, such burning is wont to depart.

VERS. 40.-- *And when the sun was down. [n: (BEDA.)]* The setting of the sun is the passion of Christ, through which more possessed by demons are healed than he had healed sick people while living before in the flesh; because living in the flesh he taught few of the Jews, but after the resurrection he appeared to the Gentiles.

VERS. 41.-- *Thou art the Son of God. [n: (ID.)]* When the devil saw him wearied [0256A] with fasting, etc., *as far as* because, *if they had known the Lord of glory, they would never have crucified him [n: (Matth. I)]*.

VERS. 42.-- *And when it was day.* Mystically: in the manifest light of the resurrection he is sought by the companies of believers; and being found in the desert of the Gentiles, he is held back, that he may not go away.


## CHAPTER V.

VERS. 1.-- *And it came to pass.* Neither by time nor by place is the crowd restrained from its zeal for being healed; evening presses on, and yet it follows; the lake meets them, and the crowd presses, and therefore he went up into Peter's ship.

*He stood beside. [n: (BEDA.)]* The lake signifies the present world. But the Lord is now no longer in the lake, as being now no longer in passible flesh, but beside the lake, because in that flesh in which he suffered he came to the stability of perpetual [0256B] rest.

VERS. 2.-- *He saw two ships. [n: (ID.)]* The two ships, mystically, signify the circumcision and the uncircumcision, which he saw, because in each people *he knoweth who are his,* whose hearts comes forth from the waves of the world to the tranquillity of the life to come, as it were to the shore, by living mercifully.

VERS. 3.-- *He asked him. [n: (ID.)]* That he first asks the ship to be drawn back a little from the land, etc., *as far as* it pertains to the more remote Gentiles to whom it was afterwards preached.

VERS. 4.-- *Launch out into the deep. [n: (AMBR.)]* Although it is commanded to others that they let down their nets, etc., *as far as* or the Church is instructed and armed from the flowing gifts of the Holy Ghost.

VERS. 5.-- *At thy word,* etc. [n: (BEDA.)] Unless by the word of grace [0256C] the instruments of disputations have been let down, etc., *as far as* even amid the scandals of persecutors.

VERS. 7.-- *And they filled. [n: (ID.)]* This filling grows even unto the end of the world, etc., *as far as* and such humility deserves the merciful consolation of the Lord.

VERS. 10.-- *From henceforth. [n: (ID.)]* This pertains specially to Peter himself, to whom is expounded what the catch of fishes signifies. But as then he took fishes by nets, so at some time he shall take men by words, wherein Peter is a type of the whole Church.

VERS. 12.-- *In one of the cities.* Here two comings into Capharnaum are noted. The first, when John had been imprisoned, when he wrought those three miracles in succession; the second, when he came down from the mountain, the apostles having been chosen, when this miracle of the leper is wrought.

[0256D]VERS. 13.-- *He touched.* Because of humility, that he might teach us to despise no one for any blemish of the body. He touches, not disdaining; he commands, not distrusting; he adds the testimony of the work. *I will.* He says it because of Photinus, he commands because of Arius, he touches because of Manichaeus.

VERS. 14.-- *He charged him. [n: (BEDA.)]* He bids it be kept silent, and it cannot be kept silent. Not that he willed something and could not, but he gives an example that his own should wish to lie hidden in the great things they do, yet that they may profit others, be published against their will.

VERS. 15.-- *And the crowds came together,* etc. [n: (BEDA.)] The healing of this man drives many crowds to the Lord. For that he might show himself healed within and without: he does not keep silent the benefit received, even when bidden. But [0257A] Mark also says, discharging the evangelist's office: *Straightway going forth, he began to publish and to spread abroad the word [n: (Marc. I)]*. [var: Vulg. Mc 1:45 reads *At ille egressus coepit praedicare, et diffamare sermonem*, "But he going out began to publish and to spread abroad the word."]

VERS. 16.-- *And he was praying.* When he prays, he forms the contemplative life; when he heals, the active.

[n: (BEDA.)] He heals as God, he prays as man. In the city, etc., *as far as* let him speak silently through inward desires.

VERS. 17.-- *And it came to pass.* etc., [n: (ID.)] Where he sat and taught Luke passed over for the sake of brevity, etc., *as far as* which he had made his own not by being born, but by making it illustrious with his powers.

*And there were. [n: (AMBR.)]* Among the remedies of the other weak ones, as they came together, etc., *as far as* and let the halting steps of our actions be reformed by the remedy of the heavenly word.

VERS. 18.-- *Men carrying.* Allegorically: that is, raising to higher things the mind of men, though sluggish through the weakness of [0257B] the outward man; by whose supports in turn one is taught both to lift himself up and to humble himself more easily before Jesus, worthy to be seen by the Lord's regard. For God looks upon humility.

*And they sought,* etc. [n: (BEDA.)] They desire to offer, but with the crowd interposed they are shut out, because often the soul, etc., *as far as* because it is by the devotion of faith that one descends to Christ's humility, and comes to the knowledge of Christ.

VERS. 19.-- *And through the tiles. [n: (ID.)]* The house of Jesus is described as roofed with tiles, because under the contemptible veil of the letter, if there be present a teacher to unlock divine things, the power of spiritual grace is found. And that he is let down with his bed signifies that Christ must be known by a man still set in this flesh.

[0257C]VERS. 20.-- *Whose faith. [n: (BEDA.)]* Each man's own faith avails much, since through another's faith a man has been saved within and without.

[n: (ID.)] This healing of the paralytic signifies the salvation of a soul sighing after God, etc., *as far as* which are prudence, fortitude, temperance, justice.

*Man, they are forgiven,* etc. Thou art a man, a sinner; I am God, who forgive sins, who by the merit of others release thy sins for thee. Let him therefore who is burdened with sins call in the Church to pray for him.

Because for the fault of the soul the body also was made infirm, therefore, about to heal the body, he first heals the soul.

VERS. 21.-- *Who can forgive?* No one forgives sins but God, who forgives also through those to whom he has given the power of forgiving. Since therefore Christ [0257D] forgives, he is truly proved God. They render true testimony to God, but by denying the person of Christ they are deceived. For faithlessness can confess, it cannot believe. And so testimony is not wanting to the Godhead, faith is wanting to salvation. But God, willing to save sinners, both shows himself God by the knowledge of hidden things, and, answering them by the wonder of his deeds: *Why do you think evil things in your hearts?* [var: Vulg. Lc 5:22 reads *Quid cogitatis in cordibus vestris?*, "What is it you think in your hearts?"; *mala*, "evil things," stands at Mt 9:4.] By the same majesty and power by which I behold your thoughts, I can forgive men their offences. From yourselves understand what the paralytic obtains. But because you do not know spiritual grace, nor believe what has been done within, let it be proved by a visible sign that it is of no less power, that in the Son of man [0258A] you may know the hidden power of majesty whereby he can forgive sins as God.

VERS. 22.-- *But when he knew,* etc. [n: (AMBR.)] The great priest saw leprosy in the hearts of the Jews, and showed them worse than that leper whom, when cleansed, he bade offer himself to the priest. But these the high priest rejects, lest their leprosy defile others also.

VERS. 24.-- *I say to thee, arise. [n: (BEDA.)]* To arise is to draw the soul away from carnal desires. To take up the bed, etc., *as far as* by the release of the penalty understand the remission of the fault.

VERS. 26.-- *And astonishment. [n: (AMBR.)]* The unbelieving look on him as he rises, they marvel at him as he goes away, and they choose rather to fear the miracles of the divine work than to believe; for if they believed, they would not fear, but would love. *For perfect [0258B] love casteth out fear [n: (I Joan., IV)]*. [var: Vulg. 1 Jo 4:18 reads *perfecta caritas foras mittit timorem*; Migne prints *dilectio*, "love", for *caritas*, and adds *enim*.]

VERS. 27.-- *Levi. [n: (BEDA.)]* The same Levi who is also Matthew. But Luke, etc., *as far as* he was changed from a publican into an evangelist.

[n: (BEDA, AMBR.)] He left his own, who was seizing what was another's. Nor did he only leave the gains of the tolls, but he also despised the danger that could come from the princes of the world, because he had left the accounts of the tolls unsettled. And because he reserved to himself absolutely no regard or thought of this life, he deserved to be a faithful steward of the Lord's talents.

[n: (BEDA.)] By the election of Matthew is expressed the faith of the Gentiles, who before gaped after worldly things, but now refresh the body of Christ with assiduous devotion. By the pride of the Pharisees is intimated the envy of the Jews, [0258C] which is tormented at the salvation of the Gentiles.

VERS. 30.-- *Why,* etc. [n: (AMBR.)] Every question from the law, which is tenacious of justice, has not, etc., *as far as* and his walking and all that he did serve for salvation.

[n: (AMBR., BEDA.)] He who receives Christ into the inward dwelling is fed with the greatest delights of overflowing pleasures. And so the Lord enters, and reclines in the affection of him who has believed. But again the envy of the faithless is kindled, and a likeness of the punishment to come is prefigured. For while the faithful feast and recline in the kingdom of heaven, faithlessness shall be tormented with fasting. It is shown also how great a difference there is between the rivals of the law and of grace, because they who follow the law shall suffer an everlasting hunger of mind. But they who have received the word in their inward parts, refreshed by the abundance of the heavenly nourishment and fountain, [0258D] cannot hunger and thirst; and therefore they who fast in mind murmur, saying: *Why with publicans,* etc.? Sons of the devil, they pour abroad the venom of the serpent. The serpent uttered the first word, saying to Eve: What indeed did God say: *Eat ye not of every tree? [n: (Gen. III.)]* [var: Vulg. Gn 3:1 reads *Cur praecepit vobis Deus ut non comederetis de omni ligno paradisi?*, "Why hath God commanded you that you should not eat of every tree of paradise?"] These men imitate him.

VERS. 31.-- *A physician.* Christ the physician who, by a wondrous manner of healing, *was wounded for our iniquities.* By this medicine the venom of the serpent is shut out. He who uses this medicine does not remain fasting.

VERS. 32.-- *To call the just. [n: (BEDA.)]* He calls those just who, *not knowing the justice of God and willing to establish their own, are not subject to the justice of God;* [var: Vulg. Rom 10:3 reads *Ignorantes enim justitiam Dei, et suam quaerentes statuere*, "For not knowing the justice of God, and seeking to establish their own."] and, presuming upon the law, [0259A] do not seek the grace of the Gospel; but sinners, who, attending to their own evils and thinking that they cannot be justified by the law, submit themselves by repentance to the grace of Christ.

VERS. 33.-- *Why do the disciples. [n: (BEDA.)]* Matthew says that the disciples themselves asked this. But it must be known that both asked.

[n: (ID.)] Spiritually: the disciples of John and of the Pharisees fast, because they who the works of the law, etc., *as far as* he eats with sinners, that thou mayest understand the grace and the power.

VERS. 34.-- *For can you. [n: (ID.)]* As long as the bridegroom is with us and we are in gladness, neither can we fast, etc., *as far as* then shall the children of the bridegroom fast, desiring the coming of the Judge.

[0259B]VERS. 35.-- *But the days will come. [n: (ID.)]* As if he should say: You will bring it about that mourners fast, because you are, etc., *as far as* as long as the Lord dwelt in the flesh with the disciples.

*Then they shall fast,* etc. Concerning both fasts, that which is in tribulation and that which is in joy, he answers: *When the bridegroom shall be taken away,* then shall they be in sorrow and in mourning, until consolation be granted through the Holy Ghost; and when that gift has been received, the fast which is made through gladness they, being now renewed unto the spiritual life, shall celebrate. And here he treats first of the fast which pertains to the humility of tribulation. But that which pertains to the joy of the mind (when the mind is suspended toward spiritual things, and is therefore estranged from bodily foods) he signifies by the similitudes that follow, showing that [0259C] to the carnal, and for that reason still drawing the old sense with them, this kind of fasting is not fitting.

VERS. 36.-- *From a new garment. [n: (BEDA.)]* To the old garment are compared the disciples, who are still the old man. To whom the new piece, etc., *as far as* whence the Apostle: *Put off the old man with his deeds, and put on the new [n: (Ephes. IV)]*. [var: Vulg. Col 3:9 reads *expoliantes vos veterem hominem cum actibus suis*, "stripping yourselves of the old man with his deeds," and Eph 4:24 *et induite novum hominem*, "and put on the new man"; the quotation joins words from the two places.]

VERS. 37.-- *Wine.* By wine we are refreshed within, by the garment we are covered without. The garment therefore is the good works which we do outwardly, by which we shine before men. The wine is the fervour of faith, of hope and of charity, by which we are reformed within, in newness of sense, in the sight of God.

*Into bottles. [n: (BEDA.)]* They are compared also to old bottles, which are more easily burst by new wine, that is, by spiritual precepts, than they contain it. But they shall be [0259D] new bottles when, after the ascension, having received the Spirit, they shall be renewed by praying with desire of his consolation.

Otherwise: to a soul not yet renewed, but persevering in the oldness of malice, the sacraments of the new mysteries ought not to be committed. Otherwise: the old bottles are the scribes and Pharisees; the new piece and the new wine, the precepts of the Gospel, which the Jews cannot bear, lest a greater rent be made, with whom the Galatians mingled the precepts of the law, new wine into old bottles. But they suit the apostles, not those who, depraved by the traditions of the elders, are unable to keep the sincerity of Christ's precepts; whence it is subjoined: *And no man,* because to the Jews, steeped in the spittle of the old life, the precepts of the new grace were loathsome, because, heaped up with the traditions of the elders, [0260A] they were not able to perceive the sweetness of spiritual words.


## CHAPTER V. (cont.)

VERS. 38.-- *But wine.* The frailty of the human condition is laid open, when our bodies are compared to the cast-off skins of dead animals. But when the bottles have been renewed, new wines are brought in, when we keep unviolated the sacraments which we have received. These bottles, if they are full, grace preserves; if empty, moth and rust consume: therefore let them always be full.


## CHAPTER VI.

VERS. 1.-- *And it came to pass,* etc. [n: (BEDA.)] He says that the letter of the sabbath had begun to be loosed, etc., *as far as* after the second, from that on which the preceding things were said or done.

[0260B] *Through the corn,* etc. Mystically: the field is the world; the crop of the field, the fruitfulness of the human race; the ears, the fruits of the Church, with which the apostles are filled, feeding themselves on our progress.

*The disciples plucked,* etc. The disciples, not having space to eat because of the importunity of the crowds, hungered as men, but by plucking the ears they solace their want, which is a sign of a more austere life, to seek not prepared dishes but simple foods. And note that the apostles already destroy the letter of the sabbath, against the Ebionites, who receive the other apostles, but reject Paul as a transgressor of the law.

[n: (BEDA.)] Mystically: the disciples, hungering for the salvation of men, pass through the corn, that is, through the regions which, etc., *as far as* convicting the doctors of the law of not knowing [0260C] the law.

VERS. 2.-- *They said to them,* etc. Others say that these things were objected to the Lord himself. But they could have been objected by different persons both to the Lord himself and to the disciples, and to whomsoever it was objected it looks chiefly to him. *On the sabbaths.* The sabbaths signify keepings of feast; either those which are kept in this world by those who give themselves to the superstitions of the Jews; or those which shall be kept in the world to come, when in perpetual solemnity we shall eat the good things of the earth.

VERS. 3.-- *Have you not read this,* etc. [n: (BEDA.)] How is that objected to the Lord as a crime, which in a servant is not held for a crime? The true king, etc., *as far as* all the sons of the Church are priests, anointed with the Holy Ghost that they may offer themselves.

VERS. 4.-- *He went into the house of God.* Great is the grace [0260D] of hospitality: with the peril of death set before it, the mind of the priest does not turn the guest away. And so we ought to take upon ourselves the perils of others. But the true priests do not shut out the true David from the hospitality of the mind, not even for the peril of death.

VERS. 6.-- *A man. [n: (BEDA.)]* Mystically this man signifies the human race dried up in the fruitfulness of good work, through the hand stretched out to the tree in the first parent, which the innocent hand stretched out on the cross heals. And well was the hand withered in the Synagogue, because where there is a greater gift of knowledge, there the transgressor lies under a greater fault.

VERS. 7.-- *They watched. [n: (ID.)]* Because he had excused, by the probable example of the master, the destruction of the sabbath which they were charging against the disciples, [0261A] now, watching the master himself, they wish to calumniate him, that if he heal not, they may accuse him of cruelty or of weakness; if he heal, of transgression.

VERS. 9.-- *I ask you. [n: (BEDA.)]* Forestalling the calumny which they were preparing for themselves, he reproves them, etc., *as far as* God is not the author of evil, but those whom he does not save he is said to destroy.

VERS. 10.-- *Stretch forth. [n: (ID.)]* The weakness of an unfruitful soul is cured by no better order than by the bounty of alms; whence it is said: *Let not thy hand be stretched out to receive, and drawn back to give [n: (Eccles., IV)]*. For he stretches out his hands to God in vain, being about to pray for his sins, who does not stretch these out to the poor according to his power.

VERS. 11.-- *Folly.* Because they were moved by envy alone, [0261B] for there was no fault either in a man's stretching out his hand, or in Jesus' healing. It was great folly to plot the death of him whose benefits they very greatly needed.

VERS. 12.-- *He went out into a mountain to pray.* The mountain on which he chooses and teaches the apostles signifies the height of justice, because they were to be instructed and to preach, that they might not remain among the weak, but be raised to things above. So also the law was given on a mountain. He leads the apostles up more eminently, that from the mountain the mountains may first receive the peace to be announced to the people. So also Moses, alone understanding the law spiritually, ascended the summit of the mountain on which God was.

VERS. 13.-- *Disciples.* Of whom he chose twelve, whom he named apostles, that is, sent in his own place; whence: *As the Father hath sent me, I also send you [n: (Joan. XX)]*. Ascending into the mountain he calls to himself, he chooses whom he wills, [0261C] because it is not of their zeal but of divine grace that they were called to the apostleship; whence: *You have not chosen me, but I have chosen you [n: (Ibid., XV)]*. He chose not the wise, not the rich, not the noble, lest he should seem to have chosen them for prudence, or to have redeemed them with riches, or to have drawn any to grace by the authority of power and nobility.

VERS. 14.-- *He surnamed.* Not then for the first time, but long before, when he is said to have been brought by Andrew: *Thou shalt be called Cephas, which is interpreted Peter.* But Luke, wishing to name the names of the twelve apostles, since he had need to say Peter, wished briefly to hint that he was not so called before; but that the Lord so surnamed him, that it should not be thought done by chance, but that from the change the sacrament of the rock might be commended to the hearers.

[0261D]VERS. 15.-- *Matthew.* Matthew, for humility's sake, places himself in the order beneath his fellow Thomas, though by the other evangelists he is set before him.

VERS. 17.-- *And coming down,* etc. The Lord, about to choose apostles, went up into the mountains; about to teach the crowds, he returns to the plains, because the crowds are not able to see Christ except in what is lowly, and rarely is it found either that a crowd follows the Lord to higher things, or that any weak man is cured on the mountain; but when the fever of lusts has been quenched and the light of knowledge kindled, each one step by step goes up to the height of the virtues.

*The sea coast.* Not from the neighbouring sea of Galilee, because they do not set this in the place of the miracle, but it is named from the great sea. In which Tyre and Sidon also [0262A] could be comprehended. But because they are cities of the Gentiles, they are deliberately set down by name, that it may be intimated how great is the power of Christ, which stirs even foreign cities.

VERS. 18.-- *That they might hear.* If we say that there are two discourses, he went up into the mountain, and had his disciples there; and afterwards he came down and made this one to the crowd. If we say there was one, it must be understood thus, that he went up into the mountain, and after a little came down, and there made the discourse, where there was a level place.

VERS. 19.-- *To touch him.* He touches Christ who faithfully believes in him. He is touched by Christ who is made firm in him. He who so touches and is touched is healed by the power of his spirit.

VERS. 20.-- *And he, lifting up,* etc. Although he speaks generally to all, he lifts his eyes specially upon the disciples, [0262B] that they who receive the word of God with the intent ear of the heart may more widely receive the light of the inward savour; to whom, sitting on the mountain, he opens his mouth, that he may utter great things upon them; standing on the plain he directs his eyes, that they may patiently understand what they hear. *Blessed are the poor,* etc. They who cannot yet climb the citadel of consummated virtue are meanwhile to be cherished with the beatitude of a general perfection, that little by little advancing to better things, while they gladly listen to the Lord standing on the plain, they may at some time loftily ascend to him sitting on the mountain. For those upon whose hearts he still presses to tame them, these he addresses as it were standing. Those whom he finds exercised by long endeavour and now teachable, to these he imparts mystical things as one resting at ease.

[n: (BEDA.)] What in Matthew is fully said by eight [0262C] beatitudes in the sermon of perfection to the apostles on the top of the mountain, this in Luke, etc., *as far as* but because he excelled in faith, by faith he deserved the primacy before the rest.

VERS. 21.-- *You that now hunger.* He who hungers has compassion on him that hungers: by compassion he gives freely, by giving freely he becomes just; he makes himself equal to his inferiors, he shuts out guile, he seeks the truth. *You that now weep.* He who for the riches of Christ's inheritance, for the bread of eternal life, for the hope of heavenly joys, desires weeping, hunger and poverty, is blessed; much more blessed he who does not fear to keep these virtues amid adversities.

VERS. 23.-- *Rejoice and be glad.* Amid the hatreds of hearts, amid the reproaches of tongues, amid the hands of persecutors, [0262D] be conversant with a gladder heart, in view of the reward on high. *For according to these things.* By the example of the prophets he strengthens them, because those who speak the truth are wont to suffer persecution.

VERS. 24.-- *But woe,* etc. Matthew on the mountain describes only the beatitudes of the upright; but Luke on the plain describes also the woe of the wicked, because hearers still untrained are to be driven to good things by threats and terrors, while for the perfect it is enough to be invited by rewards. *But woe.* Since above the kingdom of heaven is said to be the poor's, it appears by the contrary that he estranges himself from this kingdom who seeks his consolation in temporal things. Nor are riches so much in fault as the love of riches.

[0263A]Mystically: the rich man is the Jewish people, or the heretic, or the philosopher of the world, who, delighted with the abundance of words and the patronage of eloquence, having gone beyond the simplicity of the true faith, have laid up useless treasures, disputing according to the usage of the world about the generation of Christ; who in the world to come shall recognize the neediness of their faith, and, wasted with eternal fasting, shall know that the food of faithlessness which they belch forth in the present is the cause of so great a punishment. And there shall be a time when they shall bewail their laughter. To whom it is well said: *Woe, when men shall bless you;* because while here they please the powerful, they subject themselves to a perpetual curse, because as the poor, the hungry, the weeping are proved by the wickedness of the evil, so those given to riches, to feasts and to laughter, by the evil retinue of those who fawn upon them, are cherished unto a greater punishment.

[0263B]VERS. 25.-- *You that are filled,* etc. You who among the rich abound the more, like the rich man who was clothed in purple and fine linen and with feasts, who seeks a drop of water from Lazarus' finger.

*Woe to you that now laugh,* etc. Solomon: *The heart of the wise man is where there is sadness, the heart of fools where there is gladness [n: (Eccl. VII)]*; teaching that folly is to be ascribed to those who laugh, prudence to those who weep.

VERS. 26.-- *Shall bless you,* etc. A great part of the punishment of sinners is that their crimes are not only not reproved, but are moreover praised as though well done.

VERS. 27.-- *Love your enemies.* When it has been said what they can suffer from enemies, he adds how they ought to bear themselves toward those enemies. And here the Church is not fed with milk, but strengthened with the stronger food of charity. It is not [0263C] enough not to hate, but what is beyond human nature is commanded, to love. And whereas the law commands a return of vengeance, the Gospel bestows charity for enmities, kindness for hatreds, prayers for curses, patience for persecutors, and even the grace of recompense for those who hunger.

VERS. 28.-- *Pray for them that calumniate you.* That the prophets seem to imprecate, these are not the wishes of men desiring it, but foretellings of things to come: for they themselves prayed for their enemies.

VERS. 29.-- *And to him that striketh thee.* Since charity is patient, it ought to sustain the patient bearing of him that beats; and since it is kind, it ought not to answer curses. If it *seeketh not her own,* it ought not to resist him that plunders; if it *envieth not [n: (I Cor. XIII)]*, it ought not [0263D] to hate an enemy. *Offer them,* etc. The physician of souls commands those whom he instructs to heal their neighbours to bear all things that can avail for the salvation of their neighbours. And it pertains to mercy that, as from sick and frenzied sons, they should suffer many things, if their health require it, until the infirmity pass. What is so wonderful as to offer the cheek to him that strikes? Nay, every assault of the angry man is broken, wrath is calmed; and through patience he is invited to repentance. *A garment.* What is said of the garment and the coat is to be done also in other things which by any right we call temporally our own. For if this is commanded concerning necessary things, much more is it enjoined to despise superfluous things.

[0264A]VERS. 30.-- *Ask not again.* Because *charity is patient, is kind [n: (I Cor. III)]*; it not only bears bravely the injuries of an enemy, but also kindly anticipates the favour of a friend, that they may be drawn by other kindnesses.


## CHAPTER VI. (cont.)

VERS. 31.-- *And as you would.* Lest he should seem to dissolve the law, he keeps in benefits the return which he neglects in injuries. Nor does he say, as they do, but *as you would that men should do to you, that you also should do to them,* that the return may be the fuller, since the act is matched with the wish. And now extend the works of charity not only to friends, but even to enemies, that they may begin to be friends; and first extend the affection of love, and afterwards the works of charity.

VERS. 32.-- *And if you love.* The more eminent your degree of profession, the more needful it is that the care [0264B] of virtue be more abundant, that you embrace in the bosom of charity even those who do not love.

There are two kinds of benefit: either when we give kindly what we give, or when we lend to one who will repay.

VERS. 35.-- *But yet,* etc. After he has reproved as unfruitful the love and mutual benefit of sinners, he now shows how these are to be done fruitfully by the faithful, as if he should say: Although such love and mutual benefit profit not sinners, yet do you love, not only by the leading of nature, as they love friends, but by a higher degree of virtue, your enemies. The philosophers divide justice into three parts: one toward God, which is called piety; another toward parents, or the rest of the human race; the third [0264C] toward the dead, that the due rites of burial be paid to them. But the Lord, having gone beyond the oracle of the law and the summit of prophecy, extended the office of piety even to those who have done hurt.

*Hoping for nothing thereby: and it shall be.* Not fixing the hope of a reward in man. For whether he repay what you have lent, God will nevertheless repay also, at whose bidding you did it; or whether he repay not, your inheritance shall be for ever.

VERS. 36.-- *Be ye therefore merciful,* etc. He rains upon the evil, the earth does not deny its produce, the same sun enlightens the good and the evil.

Mystically: he waters the people of the Jews with prophetic rains, and upon those not deserving it the ray of the eternal sun has shone. If therefore they have believed, be merciful to them, by teaching and by lifting them up, and do not rashly judge, [0264D] lest anyone conscious of his own offence be compelled to pass sentence upon another.

VERS. 37.-- *Judge not.* There are certain middle and uncertain things, in what mind they are done, because they can be done well and ill. We know not either what he will be like who now appears evil, of whose correction to despair, and to reprove him as though cast off, is rash.

Concerning open things which cannot be done with a good mind, it is permitted us to judge.

VERS. 38.-- *For with the same measure.* It can be taken generally of all things which are done by mind, by hand, by tongue, because God will render according to the works of each.

[0265A] *For with the same, [n: (AMBR.)]* God will not repay with an unjust measure, but it is as if he should say: The very rashness of judging and the unjust measure hurts thee, punishes thee, and often not him about whom thou doest this. Or if thou judgest and measurest rightly, it profits thee, it saves thee, even though sometimes not him upon whom thou bestowest these things.

VERS. 39.-- *He spoke.* By which he invites to the giving of alms and to the forgiving of injury. *Can he.* If anger has blinded thee against a violent man: if thou shalt be covetous against him that asks, canst thou, with thy mind vitiated, heal his vice? And not only he who has done thee injury, but thou also who knowest not how to bear it, shalt be guilty. But if his wickedness finds thee tranquil, both he shall repent, and thou shalt be crowned for thy patience, because thou hast taken care to lead the blind man with a seeing eye, that is, with a serene heart, [0265B] to the light.

VERS. 40.-- *As the master.* God does not avenge his own injuries, but by bearing with persecutors he willed to make them milder; the disciples, who are men, ought to imitate this rule of perfection.

VERS. 41.-- *And why seest thou,* etc. Truly a sinner is not able to chastise a sinner, because they who, forestalled by pride, or by hatred, or by some other vice, judging these things light or nothing, gravely rebuke those whom they see disturbed from the state of the mind, either by anger or by some slight sin, such men as these love rather to blame and to condemn than to correct and to amend. *Thou considerest not.* The open eye sees ill, namely its own boasting, while it wishes to heal others, but being blind it is not able to look upon itself, while in this it falls the worse.

[0265C]VERS. 42.-- *Hypocrite.* If thou wilt reprove anyone, first see whether thou art like him. And if thou art, groan alike with him, and be not willing that he should obey thee, but admonish him to strive alike. But if thou art not like him, yet because thou once wast, or couldst have been, condescend, and reprove not out of hatred but out of mercy. Rarely therefore, and not without great necessity, are rebukes to be applied, and not save out of regard for God, when the beam has been removed from the eye.

For these men undertake to accuse all things out of hatred or envy, and wish to seem counsellors, without the example of their own amendment. But they ought first to take away the beam of envy, or of malice, or of dissimulation, from the hidden place of their heart, that they may be able to cast out the mote of anger, or of any fault of envy, from their brother's eye.

[0265D]VERS. 43.-- *There is no tree,* etc. A tree good or bad is not nature, which was created good by God in all things, but a good or a bad will. The fruits are the works, which can neither be good of a bad will, nor bad of a good will. *Nor a tree.* If thou wilt have true justice and not feigned, fulfil in deeds what thou showest in words, that thou mayest be a good tree, and be adorned with good fruits. Because although the hypocrite feign, he is not good who does evil works; and if he reprove the guiltless, he is not therefore evil who does good works. And note that here the discussion is of manifest things.

VERS. 44.-- *For every tree.* The evil are known by their own fruit, while they oppress the good, and blaspheme God by their deeds if not by their words: and chiefly they are discerned by their impatience [0266A] in the time of adversity. For fasts and prayer and things of this kind belong to the feigning as also to the good: but the sheep ought not to lay aside their own fleeces, even if sometimes wolves cover themselves with them. *Is known.* Not by the garment, but by the works which are done by them, are they known. Because as the thorn does not make a grape, so a good tree makes what is good, a bad tree what is bad, and not the contrary. *For neither of thorns.* Not of thorns, that is, of the cares of the world, figs, that is, the sweetness of the resurrection, nor of the bramble, that is, of the pricking of vices, a grape, that is, the fruit of the soul. Which, as a grape near to earthly things is corrupted, in higher places ripens. Or from the flesh, which brings forth thorns and thistles, we do not gather Christ, who hung upon the tree like a grape.

The thorn and the bramble, to the heretics, from whom [0266B] no wise man will be able to find holiness or virtue, but they tear and bloody those that draw near. Or the thorns and brambles are the cares of the world and the prickings of vices. The grape and the fig, the sweetness of the new manner of life which Christ hungers for in us, and the fervour of love which gladdens the heart of man.

Not of thorns and brambles a grape or a fig, because the mind still pressed by the custom of the old man can indeed feign, but cannot bear the fruit of the new man. But if at any time the deeds or words of the evil profit the good, the evil do not do this, but it comes of them by the counsel of God.

VERS. 45.-- *Bringeth forth good.* He loves his enemy. He gives to everyone that asks, and things of this kind.

*Bringeth forth evil,* etc. He hates his friend, he takes away what is another's, [0266C] he does not give his own, and things of this kind, which God judges according to the intention of the heart. And truly, because it proceeds from a good or a bad heart, the fruit ought to be judged good or bad, because out of the abundance of the heart the mouth speaks what lies hidden within, that is, the effect proceeds outwardly, as well in words as in deeds. This is as regards the divine examination, to which the mouth of the heart speaks, because he is not ignorant from what intention words proceed. Because even the words which outwardly seem good, because they proceed from an evil root, he judges not to be good; whence he subjoins: *And why call you me Lord?* As if he should say: Why do you boast that you put forth the leaves of a right confession, who show no fruits of good work? *Out of the abundance.* By the speech of the mouth the Lord signifies all the things which are brought forth from the heart by act or by thought. [0266D] For a word is wont to be put for a deed; whence: *There was no word which he did not show them [n: (Isa. XXXIX)]*.

VERS. 46.-- *And why,* etc. This calling seems the fruit of a good tree, but because it does not proceed from the fatness of charity, God judges it not good. But what the true discernment of good or of evil fruits may be, he sets forth under another figure:

VERS. 47.-- *Everyone that cometh,* etc. After he has long disputed of the openly evil, and of the truly and the feignedly good, he likens good hearers of the word to himself, the evil to the devil. Because as Christ builds, instructs and governs one Church out of the various persons of men, being about to lead it into life eternal; so the good hearer builds for himself by various virtues a dwelling on high, [0267A] being about to rejoice in the world to come with Christ at its dedication.
